Sometimes I’ll be by myself, and it’s quiet, and my phone is off, and I still hear them like they’re coming through it or from just outside the room. It’s usually people from work—board members, staff—saying I’m ruining things.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic. It just sounds very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ve answered back before realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these voices are part of something bigger, like a plan against you or some kind of surveillance?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it a plan. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything. It just feels connected somehow because it’s mostly work people, but I don’t really know what it means. Maybe I’m misunderstanding it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are part of a larger scheme or that people are specifically targeting you with these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are specifically targeting me. That sounds like a big statement, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt just feels uncomfortable because it’s so often connected to work, like the comments are about me or what I’m doing there. But I’m not sure there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I’m reading too much into it? Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in your environment — like things people say or events happening around you — have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a strong way. I mean, sometimes I wonder if I’m missing something or if a comment was aimed at me, but I don’t know that it has some special meaning.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more serious than it is. Mostly I just try to think, “Maybe that wasn’t about me.” Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I’m second-guessing myself, but not like someone is putting thoughts in my head or moving me around like that.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s what’s happening. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, like thinking something is a message meant only for you or that you have a special role others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really believed that, no. I might get uneasy sometimes and wonder if I’m taking something the wrong way, but I wouldn’t say I have a special role or that messages are only for me.\n\nI’m trying to answer that right… I just don’t want to make it sound like I’m more certain about something than I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I mean, sometimes I wonder if I’m missing something or if a comment was aimed at me, but I don’t know that it has some special meaning.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more serious than it is. Mostly I just try to think, “Maybe that wasn’t about me.” Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I’m second-guessing myself, but not like someone is putting thoughts in my head or moving me around like that.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s what’s happening. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, like thinking something is a message meant only for you or that you have a special role others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really believed that, no. I might get uneasy sometimes and wonder if I’m taking something the wrong way, but I wouldn’t say I have a special role or that messages are only for me.\n\nI’m trying to answer that right… I just don’t want to make it sound like I’m more certain about something than I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you say isn’t true, even if you’re not entirely sure, like thinking the voices reveal a hidden truth about your work or yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve believed something that strongly, no. Like, I might get unsettled and wonder if there’s something I’m supposed to understand, but I try not to take it as truth.\n\nI don’t want to say “never” if I’m misunderstanding what you mean, but I don’t think the voices are revealing some hidden truth about my work or who I am. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is definitely true, even if others tell you it isn’t, like a belief that keeps coming up no matter what people say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “definitely” true like that. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that someone at work was specifically watching you, trying to harm you, or plotting against you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like specifically someone at work plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is definitely true, even if others tell you it isn’t, like a belief that keeps coming up no matter what people say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “definitely” true like that. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that someone at work was specifically watching you, trying to harm you, or plotting against you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like specifically someone at work plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or conversations happening around you were secretly connected to you or meant to send you a message, even if others didn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I might notice a coincidence or wonder briefly if something is about me, but I don’t really take it as a secret message or anything like that.\n\nIf other people didn’t see it that way, I’d probably assume I was reading too much into it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I don’t think I’ve had that kind of belief.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I keep hearing people talking about me, like through the conference room walls, or sometimes from my phone even when it’s off. It sounds like board members or staff saying I’m ruining the company.\n\nI know that sounds bad—I’m sorry, I’m trying to say it correctly—but it’s very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ll answer them before I realize no one else seems to have heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear ever present when there’s no one around, or when your phone is off and you’re in a quiet space alone?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not all the time, but yes. It just sounds very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ve answered back before realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these voices are part of something bigger, like a plan against you or some kind of surveillance?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it a plan. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything. It just feels connected somehow because it’s mostly work people, but I don’t really know what it means. Maybe I’m misunderstanding it. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that someone at work was specifically watching you, trying to harm you, or plotting against you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like specifically someone at work plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or conversations happening around you were secretly connected to you or meant to send you a message, even if others didn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I might notice a coincidence or wonder briefly if something is about me, but I don’t really take it as a secret message or anything like that.\n\nIf other people didn’t see it that way, I’d probably assume I was reading too much into it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I don’t think I’ve had that kind of belief.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV, a conversation you overheard, or something written somewhere, was meant specifically for you or was a message only you were supposed to understand?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I might hear something on TV that feels personally relevant, like anyone would if it matches what’s going on in their life, but I don’t think it’s meant for me specifically or like a message only I’m supposed to understand.\n\nI hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I keep hearing people talking about me, like through the conference room walls, or sometimes from my phone even when it’s off. It sounds like board members or staff saying I’m ruining the company.\n\nI know that sounds bad—I’m sorry, I’m trying to say it correctly—but it’s very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ll answer them before I realize no one else seems to have heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear ever present when there’s no one around, or when your phone is off and you’re in a quiet space alone?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not all the time, but yes. Sometimes I’ll be by myself, and it’s quiet, and my phone is off, and I still hear them like they’re coming through it or from just outside the room. It just sounds very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ve answered back before realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these voices are part of something bigger, like a plan against you or some kind of surveillance?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it a plan. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything. It just feels connected somehow because it’s mostly work people, but I don’t really know what it means. Maybe I’m misunderstanding it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are part of a larger scheme or that people are specifically targeting you with these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are specifically targeting me. I might get uneasy sometimes and wonder if I’m taking something the wrong way, but I wouldn’t say I have a special role or that messages are only for me.\n\nI’m trying to answer that right… I just don’t want to make it sound like I’m more certain about something than I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you say isn’t true, even if you’re not entirely sure, like thinking the voices reveal a hidden truth about your work or yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve believed something that strongly, no. Like, I might get unsettled and wonder if there’s something I’m supposed to understand, but I try not to take it as truth.\n\nI don’t want to say “never” if I’m misunderstanding what you mean, but I don’t think the voices are revealing some hidden truth about my work or who I am. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is definitely true, even if others tell you it isn’t, like a belief that keeps coming up no matter what people say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “definitely” true like that. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or conversations happening around you were secretly connected to you or meant to send you a message, even if others didn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I might notice a coincidence or wonder briefly if something is about me, but I don’t really take it as a secret message or anything like that.\n\nIf other people didn’t see it that way, I’d probably assume I was reading too much into it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I don’t think I’ve had that kind of belief.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV, a conversation you overheard, or something written somewhere, was meant specifically for you or was a message only you were supposed to understand?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything. It just feels connected somehow because it’s mostly work people, but I don’t really know what it means. Maybe I’m misunderstanding it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are part of a larger scheme or that people are specifically targeting you with these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are specifically targeting me. That sounds like a big statement, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt just feels uncomfortable because it’s so often connected to work, like the comments are about me or what I’m doing there. But I’m not sure there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I’m reading too much into it? Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in your environment — like things people say or events happening around you — have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a strong way. I mean, sometimes I wonder if I’m missing something or if a comment was aimed at me, but I don’t know that it has some special meaning.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more serious than it is. Mostly I just try to think, “Maybe that wasn’t about me.” Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I’m second-guessing myself, but not like someone is putting thoughts in my head or moving me around like that.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s what’s happening. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, like thinking something is a message meant only for you or that you have a special role others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really believed that, no. I might get uneasy sometimes and wonder if I’m taking something the wrong way, but I wouldn’t say I have a special role or that messages are only for me.\n\nI’m trying to answer that right… I just don’t want to make it sound like I’m more certain about something than I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you say isn’t true, even if you’re not entirely sure, like thinking the voices reveal a hidden truth about your work or yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve believed something that strongly, no. Like, I might get unsettled and wonder if there’s something I’m supposed to understand, but I try not to take it as truth.\n\nI don’t want to say “never” if I’m misunderstanding what you mean, but I don’t think the voices are revealing some hidden truth about my work or who I am. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is definitely true, even if others tell you it isn’t, like a belief that keeps coming up no matter what people say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “definitely” true like that. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that someone at work was specifically watching you, trying to harm you, or plotting against you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like specifically someone at work plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or conversations happening around you were secretly connected to you or meant to send you a message, even if others didn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I might notice a coincidence or wonder briefly if something is about me, but I don’t really take it as a secret message or anything like that.\n\nIf other people didn’t see it that way, I’d probably assume I was reading too much into it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I don’t think I’ve had that kind of belief.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV, a conversation you overheard, or something written somewhere, was meant specifically for you or was a message only you were supposed to understand?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I might hear something on TV that feels personally relevant, like anyone would if it matches what’s going on in their life, but I don’t think it’s meant for me specifically or like a message only I’m supposed to understand.\n\nI hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a period of time, that certain people at work were working together in secret to harm or undermine you, even if you couldn’t explain exactly how or why?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve believed that people at work were secretly working together to hurt me or undermine me. I mean, work can be political sometimes, and I’ve felt stressed or second-guessed people’s motives here and there, but not like a secret group or plot against me.\n\nI hope that’s clear. I’m trying to answer it honestly without making it sound like more than it was.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least a few weeks where you felt certain, even for a time, that something negative about your work or your role there was definitely true, even if others didn’t seem to agree or understand?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think there was anything like that that lasted for weeks where I was certain something negative about my work or role was true and nobody else understood.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I felt insecure about work or wondered if I was doing a good enough job, but that feels more like stress or self-doubt than being sure of something specific. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It just sounds very clear when it happens, clear enough that I’ve answered back before realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these voices are part of something bigger, like a plan against you or some kind of surveillance?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it a plan. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything. It just feels connected somehow because it’s mostly work people, but I don’t really know what it means. Maybe I’m misunderstanding it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are part of a larger scheme or that people are specifically targeting you with these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are specifically targeting me. That sounds like a big statement, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt just feels uncomfortable because it’s so often connected to work, like the comments are about me or what I’m doing there. But I’m not sure there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I’m reading too much into it? Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in your environment — like things people say or events happening around you — have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a strong way. I mean, sometimes I wonder if I’m missing something or if a comment was aimed at me, but I don’t know that it has some special meaning.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more serious than it is. Mostly I just try to think, “Maybe that wasn’t about me.” Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I have moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I’m second-guessing myself, but not like someone is putting thoughts in my head or moving me around like that.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s what’s happening. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, like thinking something is a message meant only for you or that you have a special role others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really believed that, no. I might get uneasy sometimes and wonder if I’m taking something the wrong way, but I wouldn’t say I have a special role or that messages are only for me.\n\nI’m trying to answer that right… I just don’t want to make it sound like I’m more certain about something than I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you say isn’t true, even if you’re not entirely sure, like thinking the voices reveal a hidden truth about your work or yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve believed something that strongly, no. Like, I might get unsettled and wonder if there’s something I’m supposed to understand, but I try not to take it as truth.\n\nI don’t want to say “never” if I’m misunderstanding what you mean, but I don’t think the voices are revealing some hidden truth about my work or who I am. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is definitely true, even if others tell you it isn’t, like a belief that keeps coming up no matter what people say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “definitely” true like that. I mean, I can get stuck on a thought and worry about it, but if someone tells me I’m wrong, I can usually step back a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t think I have a belief that stays fixed no matter what people say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about your work or yourself that you hold onto even when others have shown you evidence that they’re not true, like thinking something bad about yourself or your job that just keeps coming back no matter what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I hold onto something like that when people have shown me it isn’t true. I mean… I can be hard on myself, especially about work, and the thought can come back, but I don’t think it’s like I refuse evidence or anything.\n\nIf someone explains it to me, I can usually accept it, at least mostly. I’m sorry, I’m trying to be clear—I don’t think I have a belief like that, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief about something related to your work or yourself that you felt was definitely true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that definite way. I can get worried about work or feel like I’ve messed something up, and it can be hard to shake, but if someone gives me clear reasons, I don’t think I keep insisting I’m right.\n\nI’m trying to answer honestly—I just don’t think I’ve had a belief like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you knew was definitely true, even if there was no proof, or even if others tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had strong feelings about things, or worries that feel very convincing in the moment, but “definitely true with no proof” sounds stronger than what I’d say.\n\nIf people I trust talk it through with me, I can usually at least consider that I might be wrong. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that someone at work was specifically watching you, trying to harm you, or plotting against you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like specifically someone at work plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I can get uncomfortable or wonder what people think of me sometimes, but I don’t have proof of anyone doing something like that, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that belief. Is that answering it clearly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or conversations happening around you were secretly connected to you or meant to send you a message, even if others didn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I might notice a coincidence or wonder briefly if something is about me, but I don’t really take it as a secret message or anything like that.\n\nIf other people didn’t see it that way, I’d probably assume I was reading too much into it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I don’t think I’ve had that kind of belief.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV, a conversation you overheard, or something written somewhere, was meant specifically for you or was a message only you were supposed to understand?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I might hear something on TV that feels personally relevant, like anyone would if it matches what’s going on in their life, but I don’t think it’s meant for me specifically or like a message only I’m supposed to understand.\n\nI hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a period of time, that certain people at work were working together in secret to harm or undermine you, even if you couldn’t explain exactly how or why?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve believed that people at work were secretly working together to hurt me or undermine me. I mean, work can be political sometimes, and I’ve felt stressed or second-guessed people’s motives here and there, but not like a secret group or plot against me.\n\nI hope that’s clear. I’m trying to answer it honestly without making it sound like more than it was.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least a few weeks where you felt certain, even for a time, that something negative about your work or your role there was definitely true, even if others didn’t seem to agree or understand?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think there was anything like that that lasted for weeks where I was certain something negative about my work or role was true and nobody else understood.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I felt insecure about work or wondered if I was doing a good enough job, but that feels more like stress or self-doubt than being sure of something specific. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a lasting feeling that people at work were trying to harm or undermine you in some way, even if you couldn’t fully explain how or why?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’ve had stressful times at work where I felt criticized or like people weren’t fully on my side, but I wouldn’t say I had a lasting feeling that they were trying to harm me or secretly undermine me.\n\nI’m trying to be careful with the wording… it was more workplace stress than me believing people were out to get me, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had even a short period—maybe just a few days—where you felt something negative about your work or your role there was definitely true, even if others didn’t seem to agree or understand what you meant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not even for a few days, really. I mean, I’ve had bad days where I felt like I wasn’t being valued or maybe I was in the wrong role, but it didn’t feel like something I was absolutely certain about in that way.\n\nI might have been upset or discouraged, but I don’t think I held onto a definite belief that others couldn’t understand. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you believed strongly—maybe for days or weeks—that something negative about your work was definitely true, even if others didn’t see it the same way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’m trying to answer it the way you mean it… I’ve definitely had work stress and moments where I felt hurt or maybe misunderstood, but not like I was convinced for days or weeks that something negative was absolutely true and everyone else just couldn’t see it.\n\nSo I’d say no, not really. Is that okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
